MENANCE Adds XCOM-Style Campaign in Latest Update

📅 2026-11-05  |  Read Source →

Michael Manning (He/Him) has been enamored with video games since he first played the Sonic Classic Collection as a child. With decades of experience in gaming and a full tour as a Gaming Journalist, Michael is ready to step up to the plate. From AAA to the smallest passion projects, his motto is that every piece of ar

When adding new content to an early access title, developers often need to strike a balance between addressing player feedback and slotting in planned additions. When done properly, adding both can not only move a game toward its final form, but also squash bugs and hiccups that they didn’t even notice without a wide player base to catch them.

MENACE is one such indie early access darling that’s been an absolute gem to follow, providing XCOM-style combat with MechWarrior flair, a beautiful fusion of grounded, high-stakes combat.

The newest Honkai title by HoYoverse is approaching its final beta test, giving players a larger taste of its auto-battler combat.

In their most recent update, the team at Overhype Studio has implemented a mountain of fixes, as well as an XCOM Long War-style extended campaign, slowing down progression to force players into a proper test of endurance on their path to victory.

MENACE has defined itself through gritty combat and long-form tactical planning, making it a perfect follow-up from the team that developed Battle Brothers. In addition to the new campaign options, the update adds two more squadmates to join the crew, Kubra Oztok and Clay Fleetwood.

While Kubra is a super solid frontliner, with the ability to deal great mental and physical damage to enemy units, Clay is a very funny special case, being a simulation expert with no actual physical combat experience.

Rather than having a normal HP value, Clay is a long-range powerhouse who will flee instantly upon taking any form of damage. He’ll also hightail it if the battle is going poorly enough, making him a ‘win more’ addition to the team more than anything.

MENACE keeps up a great tone of realistic action and drama by balancing it with genuinely funny bits, a hard duo act to perform but one that pays off immensely. In the same way that horror and comedy are shockingly intertwined, allowing players to come up for air with a good joke or two makes it easier to take the whole setting seriously.

MENACE is one such indie early access darling that’s been an absolute gem to follow, providing XCOM-style combat with MechWarrior flair, a beautiful fusion of grounded, high-stakes combat.

All this new content comes with a facelift and small additions to make the game look and run better, including an internal error log to help diagnose hiccups or crashes, even if they stem from mods. Now that’s an addition I’d kill for in a lot of heavily moddable games, and by prepping it this early, MENACE is making the world much easier for vanilla and modded fans alike.

MENACE is a title that really feels like it has a bright future thanks to the great work from the team behind it and the incredibly passionate community it has already established. When a blog post ends with a series of community-made guides being shouted out by the devs, it really feels like the title is going places.

Thanks to early access, MENACE has opened one of the most effective developer/community pipelines I’ve ever seen, avoiding the age-old scramble of launching a game and pivoting to fix community complaints after launch by stomping them out now and encouraging the hunt for more. With a strong Very Positive on Steam and more reviews flying in every day, the players clearly like what they see.

As outwardly popular as Mechs are, it's hard to find titles that really sell their weight. While Gundam-style humanoid dash machines are always fun, getting a long-form game focusing on the big industrial stomper kind of mech is a real diamond in the rough. MENACE is a title that knows what it wants to be, is willing to shift for player feedback, and is focused on how it wants to expand despite its incredibly solid current offerings. As long as the team stays on course, it's sure to be a hit.

STJV, a French union representing members of Arkane Lyon, accuses Microsoft of major interference with the project.

Drinkable Street Fighter trading cards have baffled fans predicting Pokemon & Yu-Gi-Oh versions

📅 2026-11-05  |  Read Source →

Japan has somehow found a way to combine trading cards and energy drinks, and the internet can’t quite believe what it’s seeing.

A bizarre Japanese TV segment has gone viral after showcasing drinkable trading cards featuring characters from Capcom’s Street Fighter 6.

Yes, drinkable trading cards. And no, that doesn’t mean putting cardboard in a blender.

The segment, which highlighted products predicted to become popular in 2027, featured presenters demonstrating the unusual creation by bending cards in half and drinking liquid directly from the packaging.

The footage has taken social media by storm, with one upload alone accumulating over 15 million views on X.

A Japanese news show featured drinkable trading cards in its ranking of possible hit products for 2027. Each card contains 12ml of an energy drink and the card/drink company already has a collab with Capcom's Street Fighter. pic.twitter.com/XufheWvDvB

The product, called Energy Arts, was developed by Japanese confectionery company UHA Mikakuto in collaboration with Kindai University.

Each card contains 12ml of a concentrated, caffeine-free energy drink. Rather than tearing open a packet, users simply fold the card until a small opening appears, allowing them to suck out the liquid.

The cards feature 24 different Street Fighter 6 designs, including recognizable fighters such as Ryu, E. Honda, Dhalsim, and Jamie.

According to the manufacturer’s announcement, each serving contains 1,000mg of arginine and 1,000mg of citrulline, two amino acids commonly used in supplements.

The idea is to let gamers replenish their energy without interrupting a match. UHA Mikakuto even claims the process takes just half a second, which is apparently enough time to squeeze in a drink between rounds.

The company also promotes the tiny servings as a way to reduce bathroom breaks during lengthy gaming sessions. Because nothing ruins a competitive Street Fighter match quite like needing to use the toilet.

Pokemon cards can now be delivered in 15 minutes with massive discounts on rare cards

Drinkable cards arrive in bizarre Street Fighter 6 energy drink collab

Pokemon teams up with SPAM in absurd food collab featuring Snorlax, Ditto & Charmander

Interestingly, the cards aren’t actually new. As Dexerto previously reported, they were first unveiled in January 2026 before becoming available through Japanese retailers in July.

However, their appearance on Japanese television has given the unusual collaboration a second wave of attention, with the cards appearing fifth in a ranking of products expected to take off in 2027.

Some users expect other brands to join the trend, such as Yu-Gi-Oh. “I activate Pot of Greed which allows me to take a sip of 2 energy drinks inside this card,” one joked.

“Can’t wait for Pokemon drinkable trading cards,” another chimed in.

Some expressed concern about scalping if other IPs like Pokemon got involved, while others weren’t convinced drinkable cards were a good idea to begin with.

Of course, there’s one glaring problem for collectors: actually drinking the energy shot means bending the card in half, potentially ruining the very collectible you wanted to preserve.

For anyone hoping to keep their favorite fighter in mint condition, it might be worth buying two. One for the collection and another for when you desperately need 12ml of Street Fighter juice.

Ayrton Senna's Honda NSX Sells For Record $1.1 Million At Auction

📅 2026-11-05  |  Read Source →

One of the original NSX's biggest selling points was that Honda recruited three-time Formula 1 champion Ayrton Senna to help develop the sports car. While the legendary Brazilian wasn't impressed with the NSX at first, it evolved into a mass-produced machine he eventually praised. Senna's personal 1991 Honda NSX sold for $1.17 million at RM Sotheby's auction in London last week. The sale made the late champion's car the most expensive first-generation NSX ever sold at auction, surpassing a $1.06 million mark set for a 2003 NSX-R last year.

Honda was contractually obligated to provide Senna with an NSX for his personal use. The Japanese automaker supplied three examples of the sports car, a pair in black and one in Formula Red. The red NSX, the auctioned car, was stored in Portugal and delivered to Senna when he visited the country. It's the same car that the Brazilian was photographed washing with a garden hose. The 1992 documentary "Ayrton Senna: Racing Is In My Blood" also features Senna spinning the wheels on this NSX. After Senna's death in 1994, the sports car stayed with the Portuguese dealership that owned it until 1997.

Senna's most successful period in F1 came with McLaren from 1988 until 1993. The Brazilian won three F1 world championships and 35 races over the six seasons with the British team. Honda supplied engines to McLaren, ending the partnership after the 1992 season. If by fate, McLaren conducted a preseason test at Suzuka Circuit in February 1989, just as Honda's R&D department was wrapping up its month-long NSX testing program. The engineers let Senna run laps in the sports car. According to Road & Track, Senna wasn't pleased, stating, "I'm not sure I can really give you appropriate advice on a mass-production car, but I feel it's a little fragile."

Honda went back to the drawing board and increased the NSX's chassis stiffness by 50%. The automaker even invited Senna back to test the sports car at the Nürburgring and help tune the suspension. The expert input played a significant role in the NSX's success, from marketing to the sports car's critical success. While most of us didn't have the budget to bid on Senna's NSX, Honda is doing its part to help other NSX owners keep their cars just as pristine. The Japanese automaker's heritage program now produces over 3,000 official reproduction parts to keep first-generation NSXs on the road and running smoothly.

Free RPG WendyQuest Lets You Earn Real-Life Food Coupons

📅 2026-11-05  |  Read Source →

In a surprising turn of events, the popular fast food chain Wendy's has released an official pixelated RPG of its own, which takes heavy inspiration from classic turn-based JRPGs like Final Fantasy for its gameplay loop. Offered completely free on either Mobile or Steam, the WendyQuest RPG has about 10–15 hours of gameplay to offer.

While the charming art style and hardware requirements that could run on a "baked potato" according to its Steam page are already tempting, the real incentive for playing comes from the fact that defeating bosses will grant coupons that will earn you free food in real life, albeit with a few limitations.

As shared by Dexerto on X, by progressing through the game and defeating bosses with the power of your ever-growing party, each boss you defeat will offer an item that offers "a chance to win a different IRL Wendy's item" through the use of the Wendy's App, with each boss offering the following rewards:

Each of the free food items can also only be claimed as a bonus alongside another purchase, meaning you'll have to spend at least some money in order to actually claim your free food. The real-world coupons are also notably on a first-come, first-served basis, and once they run out, you won't be able to collect them anymore.

In the mood for a JRPG but lack the time needed to play one? Then these short, excellent JRPG experiences are exactly what you need!

While the surprisingly high quality of the game comes as a surprise, this isn't even the first time Wendy's has stepped into the gaming world, as the fast-food chain also released their own official Wendy's tabletop RPG, not to mention its custom Super Mario Maker campaign themed after the familiar mascot.

While this will likely be far from the last time Wendy's releases their own officially sponsored gaming product, there's no telling if the next one will be as generous with its free real-world offerings.
